Expose these unchanged for locked rows rather than source lock-message boilerplate. Do not write descriptions, parse prose into effects, add static values, or edit generated data. Current producer values, exact player mapping, native scratch writes, source helper quotes, arithmetic, aggregates, and retained failed attempts are recorded below and in `.omp/research/spelunking-shop-price-parity-followup.md`. No implementation, tests, build, lint, formatter, WikiBot work, or commit occurred. Remaining UI feedback stays in Phase 6; no UI planning or implementation is authorized.

### Current source/runtime price evidence — 2026-10-16

- **Access:** Offline server `http://127.0.0.1:3101` reported `source: offline`, `cdpConnected`, `injected`, and `gameReady` true. `GET /game-version` returned 404; `POST /exec` with `idleon.getGameVersion()` succeeded with Jelly_Operator 1.22. Prior HTTP 500 and failed queries remain in the research artifact as actual individual failures, not persistent unavailability. No live server or connected-game claim was used.
- **Source and scratch safety:** `ShopUpgCost` in `docs/game-snippets/spelunking/Spelunk.1.22.js:8` writes `DNSM.h.SpelunkyDN` numeric scratch, applies tier multipliers there, and returns it or the saved-level formula. Its selected branch does not assign `Spelunk`, player data, or other gameplay fields. Talent `getbonus2(1,235,-1)` writes DNSM account-max scratch; inspected source shows no save/progression write. Sushi `RoG_BonusQTY` 6/27 and completed Jelly obstruction 22 branches only read source attributes. Safely called these original helpers; afterward saved levels, Amber, first username, and its Spelunking level remained unchanged. DNSM scratch is intentionally outside saved/progression inputs.
- **Matched website inputs:** The same `/cloud-save` account went through normal `initAllItems()` → `initAccountDataKeys()` → `updateIdleonData(..., isStatic=true)` processing in a fresh in-memory map. Current Cloudsave and fixture were not written. Read prepared owners: Cooking meal `SplkUpg=61.426807484784`; Sushi 6=`30`, 27=`50`; completed Jelly 22=`0`. Native values match exactly. Talent source result `getbonus2(1,235,-1)=0.24875621890547264`, `CalcTalentMAP[235]=2`; normal website factory calculated `Player.getTalentBonusAtInputs(235, player.finalTalentInputs)` for all eleven players. Only `Sludgev4` returns `0.24875621890547264`, so website maximum exactly matches native account maximum. Website `players` are sorted by `playerID`; resolve saved `charNames[0]="Sludgadin"` by matching `playerName`, yielding player ID 0 and Spelunking skill level 165. Never assume index zero means first saved username.
- **Frozen inputs and outputs:** Native and website matched inputs produce meal scale `2` and retention `0.5`. Runtime source rows use `CustomLists.SpelunkUpg` fields `[1,2,3,7,8]` for base, increment, cap, and the 9.5/6.3 exponents. Saved Amber is `4.610006779952582e+32`; levels at indices 0/51/52/65/66 are 175/100/5/-1/-1. An isolated JS evaluator used those inputs and source arithmetic order; no live levels changed. Each next-price result below equals original `ShopUpgCost` output exactly.

| Index | Saved level | Native = isolated next cost | Cost to max | Next 10 | Affordable from saved Amber |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 0 | 175 | `4.731241546291606e+30` | unbounded | `5.5699180460683645e+32` | yes |
| 51 | 100 | `9.51942414477217e+28` | unbounded | `2.0314892613403756e+30` | yes |
| 52 | 5 | `5.186263648010394e+30` | `5.140192404098864e+36` | `5.140192404098864e+36` (five remaining) | yes |
| 65 | -1 | `2.2513410263693443e+37` | `3.746822746510951e+107` (301 purchases) | `1.7714114890492712e+39` | no |
| 66 | -1 | `6.842790275590997e+36` | unbounded | `8.23477563875874e+37` | no |

- **Bulk semantics and limit:** Source has no native cost-to-max or next-10 aggregate. These values sum frozen-input source-equation prices; finite index 65 includes the first `-1` purchase then levels 0–299. Sentinel `99,999` has no cost-to-max. Results cover one offline account and selected metadata only, not all upgrade rows, connected gameplay, or other accounts.
- **Benefit semantics:** Preserve generated static descriptions and effect values for locked rows, with owned/effective totals separate. Upgrade 4's description remains truthful source text; actual player StaminaMax depends on Spelunking skill. A saved-level `-1` to `0` purchase unlocks but does not itself grant level-1 effect.
- **Arithmetic design:** The backend plan defines finite saved-level ranges, remaining-cap semantics, fixed-input next-10 and cost-to-max quotes, source arithmetic order, and a stable near-increment-1 aggregate. No generic price engine or `99,999` iteration is planned.

### Initial shop purchase follow-up — 2026-10-16 (historical first pass; current evidence above)

- **Player-facing semantics:** Source helper `ShopUpgCost(i)` returns one purchase's Amber cost at saved level, not a bundle. For saved level `-1`, it returns the fully discounted tier/base factor directly. This establishes a first-purchase price even for an unowned upgrade; source does not say that an upgrade is purchasable while locked. For levels `0+`, cost is `0.25 × factor × increment^level + level² + 5×level`.
- **Cost factor and dependency order:** Source multiplies factors in this order: talent reduction `1/(1+max(0,talent235×CalcTalentMAP[235])/100)`; meal reduction `1/(1+SplkUpg×min(2,max(1,1+floor(firstUsernameSkillLevel/50)))/100)`; row-value term `(10+savedLevel)`; tier base cost; Sushi/Jelly retention `max(0.1,1-(max(Sushi6,Sushi27)+Jelly22)/100)`; column factor `9.5^column`; row factor `6.3^row`. It then multiplies factor by `10^7` for index ≥52 and another `10^9` for index ≥66. Negative values and unsupported inputs have no general sanitization in source; the floor is explicit only in Talent, bands, and retention rules.
- **Eligibility differs from price:** `ShopUpgVisible` returns true for index 0 or when saved level at tier prerequisite index is ≥0. `ShopUpgBuyable` additionally requires Amber ≥ one `ShopUpgCost`, saved level < `ShopUpgMaxLV`, and visibility. Thus a locked row can have a computable price but cannot be bought; an already-visible but maxed row remains visible and fails buyable. Source buyable is false at cap even if balance is ample; affordability should not claim “can afford” without explicitly distinguishing purchase eligibility.
- **Finite-max and bounded bulk:** Source exposes only a per-next-level cost and maximum-level helper; no native cost-to-max or next-10 aggregate helper appears in captured function. Summing sequential `ShopUpgCost` for levels from current saved level through target−1 is a derived projection, conditional on all inputs remaining fixed. Cost-to-max requires a finite maximum and should not imply a purchase can cross locked prerequisites. Next-10 must sum at most `min(10,max-current)` purchases; at cap it is zero purchases, not an affirmative affordable action. Both aggregate interpretations need explicit UI labels as derived totals, not source game-returned totals.
- **Nonfinite and enormous values:** Native arithmetic can overflow or become nonfinite; source has no guard. The tier exponent jumps at indices 52 and 66, and cost combines exponentiation with unlocked discounts. Do not substitute clamping or fabricated finite values. Research does not establish repository conventions or consumer formatting/precision policy for Infinity/NaN or enormous cost; these remain design decisions before display. Use exact source arithmetic and reject/display unsupported results explicitly rather than call them purchasable.
- **Source vs website feasibility:** Captured source is versioned v1.22 `docs/game-snippets/spelunking/Spelunk.1.22.js:8`; its single minified body contains `ShopUpgCost`, `ShopUpgMaxLV`, `ShopUpgVisible`, and `ShopUpgBuyable`. The cost's first-name lookup indexes `GetPlayersUsernames[0]` then `PlayerDATABASE[username].Lv0[19]`; zero usernames/missing first profile is not handled in source. Website candidates and pipeline order are documented above, but talent 235 mapping and numeric meal/Sushi/Jelly parity remain unverified. An offline POST to `/exec` previously returned HTTP 500, so no source-vs-website examples, actual next cost, balance result, or runtime branch were observed.
- **Static names and level-one benefit:** Generated `SpelunkingUpgradeRepo.ts` stores intrinsic descriptions and cost/cap/requirement metadata (existing handoff lines 136–145); upgrade 4's description explicitly says “Each player gets +{ Max Stamina for every 10 LV of their Spelunking Skill!” at repo lines 73–86. Source `StaminaMax` uses upgrade 4 bonus times `floor(characterSpelunkingLevel/10)` (snippet line 8). Therefore a locked upgrade's first level can have zero immediate StaminaMax contribution for characters below skill level 10, while still being a real unlock/level and affecting eligible characters. Static descriptions are available as real source-derived game text; no authored descriptions are needed for upgrade rows. Do not claim every first level has an immediate benefit: meaning varies by upgrade and can depend on other source state. Wiki/player-facing verification was not obtained here.
- **Limits at first pass:** The initial probe had no source server observation or representative prices, so that pass deferred dynamic-price feasibility. Later read-only calls confirmed a ready source runtime and Jelly_Operator 1.22; exact Sushi/Jelly inputs and website parity remain unresolved. The current status and attempted calls are recorded in the section above and `.omp/research/spelunking-shop-price-parity-followup.md`. No source helper call, copied constants, or implementation was made.

**Evidence limits:** Retained source is Jelly_Operator v1.22 and one unpaired saved snapshot, not connected-game parity. Preserve all prior approval history, shared failures, and unobserved-state limits; no tests/build/lint/formatters were run for this planning revision.

**Research references:** Mechanic evidence: `docs/game-snippets/spelunking/Spelunk.1.22.js:1-8`; [tools and automatic use](#tools-player-selection-and-automatic-use), [elixir state](#elixirs-ownership-memorization-equipment-and-limits), [Manic, Overstim, and Trackometer](#manic-mode-overstim-and-trackometer), and [shop price inputs](#shop-next-price-and-affordability). Static records: `data/domain/data/SpelunkingToolRepo.ts`, `SpelunkingElixirRepo.ts`, `SpelunkingUpgradeRepo.ts`, and `SpelunkingChapterRepo.ts`.

**History:** The prior supervisor decision recorded in this handoff withheld approval pending externally supplied tool/elixir records and cited unobserved Manic writer timing. Human direction supersedes those blockers only; source/save, approval, and Parts 1/2 boundaries remain unchanged.

The focused account-factory test covers populated-to-absent elixir fields, partial and changed equipment/memorization state, `-1` empty slots, replaced upgrade-derived limits, prepared Chapter consumption reduction, and AUTO access through upgrade or gem purchase while enforcing account progress. Inputs use isolated in-memory `Cloudsave` copies; `latest.json` and historical fixture-pairing tests were not changed.

Its retained output is [`../../.omp/research/spelunking-part3-runtime-smoke.log`]; the temporary TypeScript runner was removed. The smoke used in-memory save copies and an empty localStorage environment shim.

**Limits:** This exercises the existing retained account snapshot, not a connected game; source evidence remains offline Jelly_Operator v1.22 and one unpaired save. The known wider Spelunking fixture-hash failure was not rerun. The reported offline source `/exec` HTTP 500 was not retried. No build, lint, formatter, TypeScript check, independent review, tracker action, staging, or commit occurred.

**Stop:** Phase 5 review history and Parts 1/2 work remain preserved. Stop before fresh independent review, acceptance, publication, or commit.

# Spelunking: research brief

## Spelunking POW Slab contribution — v1.22 account-scope research, 2026-10-16

- **Player-facing result:** Spelunking POW receives an additive percentage from this Slab bonus. Selector `6` contributes `3` percentage points per complete five Slab registrations above 1,300, only when Gaming Superbit `25` is active. The contribution scales by Mainframe bonus `15` and the common Slab multiplier. The source does not expose a player-facing name for selector `6`; do not invent one.
- **Native source:** [`Sailing.1.22.js`](../game-snippets/sailing/Sailing.1.22.js#L591-L603) defines `SlabboBonus` case `t == 6` as `1 == GamingStatType("SuperBitType",25,0) ? 3 * (1 + MainframeBonus(15)/100) * SlabboBonus_AllMulti * floor(max(0, Cards[1].length - 1300)/5) : 0`. The shared multiplier is `(1 + Meritocracy(23)/100) * (1 + LegendPTS_bonus(28)/100) * (1 + VaultUpgBonus(74)/100)` at lines 589–590. Native `POW_multi` adds `SlabboBonus(6)` to other percentage contributions before dividing that sum by 100 in [`Spelunk.1.22.js`](../game-snippets/spelunking/Spelunk.1.22.js#L8). Thus the bonus is additive inside a multiplicative POW factor, not a standalone multiplier.
- **Account scope:** The formula reads `Cards[1].length`, not a character-indexed value. It uses account-wide registered Slab entries, applies one account Superbit gate, and uses account bonus inputs. The count advances only in complete five-entry blocks after 1,300; values below the threshold produce zero.
- **Repository mapping:** [`Slab.parse`](../../data/domain/world-5/slab.tsx#L77-L112) reads `Cards1` into `rawItems`; line 80 sets `registeredItemCount` to `rawItems.length`, matching the native source's unfiltered list length. `rawObtainedCount` at line 105 instead counts filtered `lootedInfo` and is not equivalent. `Slab.getSlabBonusMultiplier()` at lines 44–48 exposes only the common multiplier. `SlabBonusesText` at lines 13–20 contains six unrelated display bonuses, and `updateSlabBonusDisplay` at lines 136–190 prepares only those six. No prepared accessor or `SlabBonus` entry exposes this Spelunking POW contribution. The existing typed ingredients include the raw registered count and multiplier, but consumer still needs prepared Superbit 25 and Mainframe 15 inputs or their existing domain APIs; do not duplicate static values.
- **Evidence limits:** This finding uses retained Jelly_Operator v1.22 source and checked-in domain code only. No runtime query was made. Source selector `6` has no verified player-facing label in retained evidence.
## Current Part 3 mechanics findings — v1.22, 2026-10-15

### Evidence boundary

- **Version and transport:** `GET http://127.0.0.1:3101/status` reported an offline source server, `cdpConnected: true`, `injected: true`, `gameReady: true`, scene `zDesertaTown`, and player `Sludgev4`. `POST http://127.0.0.1:3101/exec` evaluating `idleon.getGameVersion()` returned `{title:"Jelly_Operator",version:"1.22"}`. This is the offline game/source instance, not a connected live-game session.
- **Endpoint failure:** `GET http://127.0.0.1:3101/game-version` returned HTTP 404. The version query succeeded through the documented offline `/exec` expression interface. One bundled source request also failed because `_CaveAutoXoff` is an object, not a function; individual method-type inspection isolated callable methods and succeeded. These failures do not block the findings.
- **Source/save proof versus runtime:** `Spelunk.1.22.js:8` is the retained entire v1.22 `_customBlock_Spelunk` source function. Read-only caller `toString()` queries resolved `ActorEvents_713` methods listed above. Read-only account-attribute queries observed a loaded saved snapshot; no originating save file was paired, no fields were changed, and no gameplay action was performed. Do not call these observations connected-game parity or claim unobserved account branches.
- **Player meaning:** Wiki [Spelunking](https://idleon.wiki/wiki/Spelunking) describes selecting a tunnel and eligible characters, choosing tools inside a delve, and using shop upgrades for POW and stamina. It describes Manic as a mode with 30x stamina cost and a separate best depth through the intrinsic upgrade text. Wiki is contextual only; v1.22 source determines gates and field semantics.

### Tools, player selection, and automatic use

- **Tool identities and gates:** `CustomLists.Spelunky[13]` contains seven ordered names: Hammer, Mace, Mallet, Flail, Warhammer, Scepter, and Mjolnir. Source helper `ToolUnlocked` grants index `0` by default and indices `1–4` only with shop upgrades `14–17`; it grants no other listed index. `_event_7` selects a tool in actor-local `_GenINFO[92]`, not a cloud-save field. `_customEvent_InsideCavernStuff` consumes tool-indexed `CustomLists.Spelunky[12]` as a stamina threshold and `[11]` in tool display/effect text; exact `[11]` units remain unmapped. Existing upgrade descriptions identify tool names, radii, damage multipliers, stamina costs, and their upgrade links for Mace through Warhammer (`SpelunkingUpgradeRepo.ts:233–295`), but no typed tool catalogue exists.
- **Run-time team selection, not save state:** The pre-delve player picker uses actor-local `_GenINFO[52]`, initialized to twelve `-1` slots, clears it when changing tunnel, and accepts each character index at most once. `_event_7` permits a character only when saved `Spelunk[3][characterIndex] >= StaminaMax(characterIndex)/2`; `PlayerSelectSlots(tunnel)` reads `CustomLists.Spelunky[0][tunnel]`, whose loaded v1.22 entries range from 3 to 12. The list is temporary actor state, not cloud-save selection. Wiki's `>50%` wording is less exact than the source's `>= 50%` check.
- **Automatic-use setting and gates:** `AutoFullClearNECESSARY` reads saved `OptionsListAccount[538]`. `CanWeUseAUTO` requires either shop upgrade `12` or gem purchase `43`, and account progress `OptionsListAccount[478] >= 15`. `_event_7` also uses actor-local `_GenINFO[96]` and `_GenINFO[143]` for the AUTO control and speed; these are run/UI state, not the saved setting. The existing upgrade description says AUTO clicks rocks and gem purchase permits repeated speed increases (`SpelunkingUpgradeRepo.ts:201–215`). Do not infer that AUTO is active from its saved full-clear setting alone.
- **Website implication:** The website may show tool catalogue, unlocks, and static rules, but must not reconstruct current run's selected characters, selected tool, or AUTO activity from actor state. No current tool or team selection save field was found in queried account state. Team eligibility can be explained as a rule; it is not an account-level current team.

### Elixirs: ownership, memorization, equipment, and limits

- **Ownership and limit helpers:** `ElixirsOwned = min(12, ShopUpgBonus(23))`; `ElixirSlotsOwned = min(5, 1 + ShopUpgBonus(24))`; `ElixirMaxDupes = min(5, ShopUpgBonus(25))`; `ElixirMemorizeSlots = ShopUpgBonus(57)` (rounded by the helper). `ElixirNotUsed = min(0.6, (ShopUpgBonus(26)+ChapterBonus(3,2))/100)`. These are account progression/gates, not evidence that every owned elixir is memorized or active.
- **Distinct persistent lists:** Equipped slots are `Spelunk[7][0..4]`, with `-1` empty; `ElixirEquippedStatus` only reports whether any is filled. The event caller enforces available slots and per-elixir equipped duplicate count before writing an elixir ID to a free `[7]` slot. Memorized IDs are a separate unique list in `Spelunk[48]`; the elixir screen adds/removes IDs there and refuses additional IDs once its length reaches `ElixirMemorizeSlots`. Thus `[7]` is equipped state and `[48]` is memorized state; neither is inferred from ownership.
- **Effect eligibility:** `CanWeUseElixir` checks actor-local active effect state for elixirs `0` and `2`; the actor's `_GenINFO[107]` data is run-time state, not saved ownership/equipped state. `ElixirEffectQTY(index)` reads the numeric quantity from `CustomLists.Spelunky[17][index]`; its units/meaning vary by effect and must not be guessed from a bare number. Existing `SpelunkingElixirRepo.ts:7–54` has twelve indexed descriptions only.
- **Website implication:** Parse saved `[7]` and `[48]` separately. Do not call active actor effects owned, equipped, or memorized state. Current offline snapshot had `[7]=[0,2,6,9,7]`, `[48]=[6]`, owned count 12, five slots, four duplicate limit, and one memorize slot. These are one unpaired snapshot, not representative coverage.

### Manic Mode, Overstim, and Trackometer

- **Manic:** `ManicUnlocked` is `ShopUpgBonus(53) >= 1`. `_event_7` toggles saved `Spelunk[4][10]` between `0` and `1` only when that gate passes; `_event_5` displays the “Manic Mode” state from that field. The helper applies the mode's 30x stamina-cost factor. The helper consumes per-tunnel Manic best-depth values in `Spelunk[47]` when computing upgrade bonuses `54–56`; the repo descriptions identify Manic depth as their multiplier. The saved toggle is persistent account state, not just actor UI. Caller evidence here did not locate the exact write that records each new Manic depth, so parsing `[47]` is source-backed but its writer/record-update timing remains unobserved.
- **Overstim:** `OverstimUnlocked` requires `ShopUpgBonus(6) >= 1`. Account level is `Spelunk[4][1]`; meter fill is `Spelunk[4][2]`; threshold is `100 * 1.3^level`. `OverstimBonus_PerLV = 30 + ShopUpgBonus(6)` and `OverstimBonus = (30 + level-6 upgrade bonus) * saved Overstim level`; Amber gain multiplies by `1 + ShopUpgBonus(6)*OverstimBonus/100`. During stamina refill, if current stamina exceeds its maximum and upgrade 6 is owned, overflow adds to `[4][2]` multiplied by `1 + ChapterBonus(2,2)/100`; it then caps stamina at maximum. The Spelunking actor UI also consumes one threshold and increments `[4][1]` when fill meets threshold. These source branches establish fill/gate/benefit; they do not establish fresh elapsed-time or every refill condition.
- **Trackometer:** `TrackometerUnlocked` requires `ShopUpgBonus(59,1) >= 1`. Saved level is `Spelunk[4][8]`; saved fill is `[4][9]`; next threshold is `1000 * 1.5^level`. Its UI consumes a threshold and increments level. Upgrade effects `59–61` are each multiplied by saved Trackometer level in `ShopUpgBonus`; accepted Part 2 maximum stamina continues to use upgrade 61 times that level (`spelunking.tsx:403–405`). Do not recalculate or reopen that accepted mechanic.
- **One offline snapshot:** `Spelunk` outer length was 49. It contained Manic toggle `[4][10]=0`, Overstim level/fill `[4][1]=23` / `[4][2]=20882.36153934422`, Trackometer level/fill `[4][8]=8` / `[4][9]=20002.1875`, and Manic-depth array `[47]=[5,5,5,11,16,8,6,7,10,0,0,0,0,0,0]`. Source-derived next thresholds are `41753.90541341315` and `25628.90625`; these are values of this snapshot, not broad runtime validation.
- **Unobserved cases:** No toggle-on snapshot, boundary-level progression, threshold crossing, partial/absent array save, positive refill overflow, or alternate account was exercised. The source identifies state and branch rules; it does not validate all save versions or live parity.

### Shop next price and affordability

- **v1.22 formula inputs:** `ShopUpgCost` uses saved level `Spelunk[5][i]`, generated base cost/increment and tier coordinates from `CustomLists.SpelunkUpg[i]`, then applies Talent 235 × `CalcTalentMAP[235]`; meal `SplkUpg` scaled by the first username's Spelunking skill level in 50-level bands, capped at two; `max(Sushi 6,Sushi 27)+Jelly 22` with at least 10% cost retained; and tier factors `9.5^column` and `6.3^row`. Index `52+` multiplies the discount factor by `10^7`; index `66+` multiplies it by `10^9`. For a saved level of `-1`, the helper returns that factor directly; otherwise price is `.25*factor*increment^level + level² + 5*level`. `ShopUpgBuyable` compares base Amber `Spelunk[4][0]` to price and also checks saved level below maximum and `ShopUpgVisible`.
- **Website input feasibility:** Current domains contain candidate producers for every discount family: `Player.getTalentBonusAtInputs`/account talent calculation (`data/domain/player.tsx:311–318`, `data/domain/talents.tsx:68–75`), meal 68's `SplkUpg` via Cooking (`data/domain/data/MealRepo.ts:553–559`, `data/domain/cooking.tsx:524–525`), Sushi 6/27 (`data/domain/world-7/sushi.tsx:308–309`), Jelly obstruction 22 (`data/domain/world-7/jellyOperator.tsx:279–282`), and first saved player's Spelunking level. This makes the inputs potentially available without new WikiBot data. The exact source mapping still needs to establish whether `CalcTalentMAP[235]` matches the website's account-talent calculation and preserve the first-username level rule. Therefore dynamic price/affordability is feasible in principle but not yet verified; do not display it until those conversions and special negative-level/first-purchase behavior match v1.22 outputs.

### Exact static-data prerequisite

Current repositories suffice for shop upgrade names/descriptions and cost/cap/requirements (`SpelunkingUpgradeRepo.ts`; model fields in `SpelunkingUpgradeModel.ts`). Existing generated elixir rows suffice only for the twelve index/description pairs (`SpelunkingElixirRepo.ts:7–54`). The source helper's numeric `CustomLists.Spelunky[17]` effect quantities are not exported as typed elixir records, and no generated tool catalogue is present. If the selected UI needs named tool/elixir entries and structured effect values, request these exact source-verified records from WikiBot owners; do not copy constants or run WikiBot here:

| Record | Required fields and types | Relationship/units | Blocked outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SpelunkingTool` | Stable `index: number` (0–6), `name: string`, intrinsic `description: string`, `staminaCost: number`, effect value/unit, and `unlockUpgradeIndex: number | null` | Source indices align with `CustomLists.Spelunky[13]` names; `[12]` feeds stamina checks and `[11]` feeds tool display/effect text, but `[11]` semantics need source mapping. Tool gates currently establish index 0 and upgrades 14–17 for indices 1–4 only; do not assign gates to 5–6 without source proof. Stamina cost is stamina units per action. Include automatic-use rules only where source-backed. | Named tool/effect cards and precise availability/eligibility/automatic-use explanation. |
| `SpelunkingElixir` | Stable `index: number` (0–11), `name: string`, existing intrinsic `description: string`, `effectQuantity: number`, `effectUnit: string`, and source-defined condition/duration/reuse or per-delve behavior | Quantity maps exactly to `CustomLists.Spelunky[17][index]`; unit/condition fields must describe that indexed effect, not assume percentages. | Named elixir entries and numeric effects; existing descriptions alone remain displayable without names or fabricated effect parsing. |

The numeric requirements above are consumer requirements, not an assertion that WikiBot already exports these schemas. Game source contains ordered tool arrays and elixir effect quantities, but generated repositories do not expose typed records for them. Tool gates, `OptionsListAccount[538]`, equipped `[7]`, memorized `[48]`, Manic/Overstim/Trackometer save fields, and shop metadata are existing game/save mappings, not missing static records.

The six additive values are upgrade 5, Alchemy `Y11`, Journal `(2,1)`, Journal `(5,2)`, Spelunking mastery slot 5, and capped native `w7a10` level contribution. Independent factors are Meritocracy 17, Legend Talent 30, selected Royal category 2, and Sushi 11. `data/domain/idleonData.tsx:404–411` settles Royal Armory and Meritocracy before Spelunking preparation. No new producer API or pipeline registration is required.

#### Standards

- **S1 — P2, section spacing has no effective owner.** `app/world-7/spelunking/content.tsx:39,56–79` puts the loaded summary, character section, tabs, and selected content inside a Fragment. Grommet's outer `Box gap="small"` inserts spacers between its direct React children, not within that Fragment. The installed implementation confirms this at `node_modules/grommet/components/Box/Box.js:149–180`. The route therefore declares a gap that does not separate its loaded sections. This conflicts with the caller-owned external-spacing convention in `docs/agents/ui-patterns.md:89–91` and the pattern-reuse intent of CONSIST-01. A future spacing change at the outer Box would still leave these boundaries unchanged. Use a real loaded-content `Box gap="small"` or equivalent explicit caller-owned spacing. No fix was made.
- **Other maintainability findings: none.** The summary export has one actual route caller. Journal and Characters no longer call it locally. All production callers use the two-tab API; the Characters tab, repeated formula tips, rejected notes, and retired visible labels are gone. No compatibility alias, copied mechanic table, generic cap framework, presentation store, extra controls, duplicate formatter, or speculative future abstraction was added.
- **Safeguards and ownership:** Required Spelunking and Players are direct accesses at content `:26–28`. Empty initialized Players and pre-hydration rendering remain supported guards, not impossible missing-domain protection. Limit/progress `undefined` at component `:74–80,86` means genuinely unbounded bonuses under domain `:237–253`. The below-full fill and `<100%` rounding serve explicit display requirements. Above-maximum stamina is observed, not hypothetical. Page arrays and player joins retain direct required access. Zero-level neutral/zero formatting is a presentation rule; accepted domain effects, limits, progress, recharge, and refill getters remain unchanged.

### Part 2 latest human feedback and Phase 6 authorization — 2026-10-14

The human selected Part 2 (#436) and authorized a fresh executor to research and plan its UI, then show a browser-rendered preview for approval. This phase is planning only. Read-only production research and disposable preview code are permitted. Preserve Part 1, accepted backend, prior approvals, intervening files, saves, and unrelated work. Part 3 remains unselected; WikiBot is outside authority.

Feedback verbatim:

1. The daily page reads / recharge / all full in sections should be above the tabs like a summary section not inside the tabs
2. Recharge / all full in should be part of one "section" in the summary for stamina so it's clear what it's talking about
3. "All full in" was just an example wording, can we try something better?
4. "Saved snapshot · Assumes unchanged recharge and no spending." and "Finite limits are approached, not reached." are not helpful labels, remove them
5. "No finite limit" is a bit verbose, just say "No soft cap"
6. The "finite limit effect" is overly verbose and hard to reason about without a math degree, simplify it and move it to the top of the page (in a tooltip as well maybe) instead of repeating it for every bar
7. "Not earned" label isn't needed, users can see when level is 0 or bonus is 0 pretty obviously. Maybe we can give it slightly lower opacity for some indication at a glance?
8. Can you look at the outposts page in royal armory and try that for the bar styles on the chapters?
9. You misunderstood my intent with the characters. I didn't want a characters tab, I want them at above the tabs (and below the summary section you will be adding).
10. Also not sure how you followed the style from sneaking/lab, this is nothing like them and their usage of `CharacterBox` style approach. Do better

**Execution boundary:** Research current Sneaking/Lab composition and Royal Armory Outposts in code and browser captures. Summary precedes a persistent eleven-character grid; both precede Tunnels/Journal tabs. Keep all six chapters and twenty bonuses. Preserve skill/EXP, current/maximum stamina, page-location details, saved-snapshot refill semantics, and correct zero-level earned effects. Centralize one simple soft-cap explanation. Return plan, comparative captures, preview references, and observed runtime limits for approval; stop before production changes.

### Part 2 reopened human feedback — Phase 3, 2026-10-14

The human selected Part 2 (#436) and authorized targeted backend-plan revision. Existing backend and UI commits are recorded above. Prior acceptance sections below remain historical.

Feedback verbatim:

1. "Read pages from one character inventory stack. Storage pages must be moved first." isn't a very helpful set of instructions, remove it
2. Looking at the competitor page, for lore they show a "cap" and a progress bar for each bonus. Is there some diminishing returns / maximum value for bonuses? if so we should indicate that on our page as well
3. "No pages in character inventories" isn't very helpful, just hide it when it's not visible. And to be honest you can just show how many pages we have in "storage" and on hover show where it's located
4. Can you try and make the lore cards more "dense". Probably smaller text size will go a long way but overall give it another try with a more dense approach
5. For the character pane, can you look at how we do Sneaking/Lab and follow a similar display model?
6. For the characters (5) will change how you do them for sure but just as additional feedback: Stamina could have just been labeled with `Stamina` and show `{saved}/{maximum}` instead of split on 2 columns, recharge per hour isn't helpful if they all have the exact same value (which they do as far as I can see), and estminated refill can maybe be a separate "summary" top level information (next to the new characters display) which shows time for all to be refilled and maybe on hover show individual players? not sure what's best here. Experiment with what works nicely with (5) and how this information should be displayed.
7. Somewhat inline with the comment above about a "summary", we can probably move the "Daily page reads" into a top level card and make it a lot more dense, right now it's pretty spread out for what little information it gives so I think it can easily become a single card on the summary. It also has redundant information like remaining / used / limit where it can simply be `8/8` and that's it.

Backend planning must distinguish asymptotic formula limits from hard level or effect caps. Include artifact scaling and unbounded bonuses. Competitor presentation is not formula evidence. This phase does not choose or implement the revised UI composition.

**Planning result:** The revised [standalone backend delivery plan](spelunking-part-2-backend-plan.md) is approved by the delegated supervisor and [published](https://github.com/Sludging/idleon-efficiency/issues/436#issuecomment-6160408409). Two serial fresh scouts located existing contracts and the original formula helper. The planning owner inspected original formula branches, the captured chapter helper and page-level assignment, and all 20 original extracted tuples. No code changed or check ran.

**Mechanics for approval:** There are two unbounded linear bonuses, sixteen `decay` bonuses, and two `decayMulti` bonuses. Positive decay formulas approach finite asymptotes but do not reach them at finite levels. The inspected level-update assignment adds gained levels without a clamp. The daily allowance caps reads, not chapter levels. `decayMulti` includes a baseline of one, so Chapter Two's two multiplier limits are `5 * A`, not `4 * A`. The current artifact factor `A` multiplies the entire effect and its limit. All 20 extracted flags enable it. Chapter Five and Six multiplier-worded bonuses use plain `decay`; do not add a baseline based on their wording.

**Exact evidence:** Original [`ArbitraryCode5Inputs.1.22.js`](../game-snippets/players/ArbitraryCode5Inputs.1.22.js#L8-L24):8–24 establishes the three formulas. [`Spelunk.1.22.js`](../game-snippets/spelunking/Spelunk.1.22.js#L8):8, `ChapterBonus`, establishes saved-level input and conditional artifact 35 scaling. [`PageRead.1.22.js`](../game-snippets/spelunking/PageRead.1.22.js#L8):8, `SPELUNKY_PAGE`, assigns the old bonus level plus `PageReadingLVgains`, without a level cap. Original tuples are retained at `tests/results/spelunking-part-2-data.json`, `extractions.chapter_metadata.result`, and match generated `SpelunkingChapterRepo.ts`:13–237. These are retained offline v1.22 source/snapshot artifacts, not newly exercised game parity.

Keep layout, wording removal, location disclosure, and formatting in the UI. Add `ChapterBonus.getEffectLimit()` and `getProgressToEffectLimit()` for request 2. Add `Spelunking.getHoursUntilAllFull()` for request 6: maximum individual snapshot estimate, not summed durations or an elapsed-time simulation. Keep these calculations with their domain owners, not in components. Use `dailyReadsRemaining / dailyReadLimit` for the compact `8/8` display.

**Unresolved blockers and limits:** No required static-data or owner-API blocker remains. Revised backend-plan approval and separate fresh Phase 4 authorization are recorded. The three new getters are present and freshly verified; the execution record above retains exact evidence. The paired real save still has only zero refill estimates; positive account refill cannot gain game-evidence coverage without suitable real paired data. Artifact-disabled behavior, performed reads/resets, automatic rollover, and connected live-game parity remain unproved. ### Part 2 UI independent review — Phase 8, 2026-10-14

- **Method and scope:** Used code-review's separate Standards and Spec axes with fresh reviewers `Part2UIReview.Part2StandardsReview` and `Part2UIReview.Part2OutcomeReview`, started serially. The integration reviewer independently inspected code, owner contracts, the corrected rendered preview, actual-route screenshots, scenario source, result/report, and complete runtime errors. Baseline was accepted backend commit `ac3df4752e3cf76202252f5d0face88db2358676`; the intervening Hole commit `f9efbcf5` was excluded. Reviewed the two scoped tracked UI diffs and the entire untracked `components/world-7/spelunking/spelunkingPart2.tsx`, not only `git diff` output.
- **Sources:** Started with `docs/agents/ui-patterns.md`; read `CODING_STANDARDS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, domain-vocabulary discovery guidance, DataRow guidance/API, ContentTabs, and the named Sneaking card reference. Loaded Impeccable's existing product/visual context for this scoped Operate surface. Reviewed [#436](https://github.com/Sludging/idleon-efficiency/issues/436) and the standalone approved UI plan against standards rather than treating approval as an exception.

#### Standards

1. **P3 — Remove impossible missing-domain checks from the route.** `app/world-7/spelunking/content.tsx:25–27` declares both initialized `Spelunking` and `Player[]` as possibly undefined, then tests their existence. This violates **DATA-01** (`CODING_STANDARDS.md:41–43`). The actual route provider calls `initAccountDataKeys` before creating the store (`lib/providers/appDataStoreProvider.tsx:25–28`). The factory registers Players and Spelunking (`data/domain/idleonData.tsx:127,170,248–260`); their initializers return an array and the domain (`data/domain/player.tsx:982–983`; `data/domain/world-7/spelunking.tsx:467–497`). Static and live loads reuse that initialized Map (`lib/stores/appDataStore.tsx:97,109`; `data/domain/idleonData.tsx:414–488`). No normal route lifecycle removes either required key. Keep the genuine empty-character/no-account check and hydration gate; use required domain types and direct access. The scenario's deliberate `map.delete('spelunking')` (`scenario-source.txt:234–239` in the actual-route packet) proves the invented branch renders, not that normal loading can omit an initialized domain.
2. **P3 — Access initialized character page stacks directly.** `components/world-7/spelunking/spelunkingPart2.tsx:25–26` adds `?? []` for a loaded player's page-stack array. This also violates **DATA-01**. Players uses IDs `0` through `charCount − 1` (`data/domain/player.tsx:982–983`), Storage builds one inventory for each actual character (`data/domain/storage.tsx:57–74`), and `Chapter.preparePageStacks` maps every inventory, including empty ones (`data/domain/world-7/spelunking.tsx:267–272,594`). Empty inventories already produce empty arrays. Remove the fallback instead of silently treating a broken required-object contract as an empty location.

- **Maintainability and plan judgment:** These are required-object contract findings, not observed wrong values. No further concrete YAGNI, DRY, MODEL-01, or calculation-placement defect was found in the selected UI. Two small views and chapter-local rendering solve current outcomes without a new store, processing hook, generic framework, forecast, or Part 3 control. Repeated fixed grid tracks do not justify another abstraction. The approved plan's required-object rule (`spelunking-part-2-ui-plan.md:94`) already supports these findings; its missing-data verification requirement does not waive DATA-01.

The two new views cover every selected outcome without Part 3 controls or new calculations.
- **Design authority:** Started with [UI patterns](../agents/ui-patterns.md), loaded Impeccable context and shape guidance, and retained the existing Operate-mode visual world. Read `CODING_STANDARDS.md` and domain-vocabulary discovery guidance. Reused Sneaking's named card traits, DataRow's row composition, ContentTabs, existing Player skill/class presentation, IconImage, TipDisplay, and established store/hydration ownership.
- **Player context:** The [Wiki Spelunking page](https://idleon.wiki/wiki/Spelunking) describes cave nodes as page/EXP sources and held inventory pages as Journal inputs. Accepted source findings P2-G1–P2-G7 establish shared reading usage, minimum individual stacks, distinct saved stamina and maxima, shared recharge, and snapshot-only refill. Generated chapter and page descriptions remain the wording authority. Neither the Wiki nor a preview replaces accepted source/test evidence.

Located the equivalent public-profile [World 7 Spelunking → Lore → Chapters](https://idleontoolbox.com/account/world-7/spelunking?profile=liiiiiiiiiiiig&t=Lore&nt=Chapters). Browser observation confirmed chapter-local artwork, effects, levels, and required pages in two-column cards. Retain only that grouping. - **Ownership decisions:** Shared reads stay above chapter cards, not in character rows. Card page locations retain separate Storage and named character inventory stacks. Each character joins to stamina by `playerID`; `playerName` supplies display identity. Earned effects require an owned level; level-zero entries show Not earned and intrinsic effect names instead of promoting formula baselines. Saved current and calculated maximum remain separate, including above-cap saves. Refills do not use elapsed wall-clock time.
- **Artwork decision from preview:** The gathering-source image API returned `Mface0` for all six caves, and those images failed to load. The scoped preview removed them instead of guessing assets or changing accepted backend/generated data. Page artwork identifies chapters; class artwork identifies characters. Cave names remain visible source text. The standalone plan records this concrete image-data limit.

This violates **DATA-01** in `CODING_STANDARDS.md:41-43`, which requires direct access to objects initialization always creates. `initAllItems` at `data/domain/items.tsx:641` initializes every generated item; its factory at `:442-476` maps each exported entry. Generated chapter page identities `Spelunking0`–`Spelunking6` all exist, including the filler page. The fallback makes a required association appear optional and silently compacts a chapter array whose consumers use chapter positions. Treat the page lookup as required and remove only this impossible-missing-item branch. Preserve the separate gathering-source filter at `spelunking.tsx:283`: the generated filler genuinely has no gathering source. This is a documented standards violation, not a runtime-parity failure or an optional smell.

No replacements or new pins appeared. Its remaining checks independently cover generated/source identities, extracted metadata and effects, saved levels, page locations, Storage-owned references, character values, and repeated processing.

The stamina input shape and Royal Armory getter serve current requirements without a new framework. Character reconciliation handles the observed zero-character initialization seam. Optional save defaults handle fields that can actually be absent. Source-required clamps are mechanic rules. No frontend row projection, authored game-description sentence, copied description table, forecast, or speculative API was added.

#### Standards

- **P3 — Tests assert aliases rather than independent consumer information.** `tests/domains/spelunking/part-2-test-helpers.ts:52,56,60` compares `chapter.name` with its backing name, a page description with the same initialized Item's description, and a bonus's metadata with its backing chapter metadata. These assertions follow the implementation's aliases. They cannot independently prove the correct chapter name, source description, or bonus information. They add test noise without extending the semantic checks already present. `docs/TESTING_IMPLEMENTATION.md:43` requires calculated results rather than incidental object representation; `CODING_STANDARDS.md:48-54` requires work that solves the current problem. Remove these redundant alias assertions, or replace them with independent source-backed consumer expectations if a missing outcome warrants them. Keep the independent generated-data comparison at line 53, source-tuple/page-requirement checks at lines 64–67, game-effect comparisons, and location-separated quantities. No fix was made.
- **Other standards conclusions:** No concrete MODEL-01, PARSE-01, UPDATE-01, or backend DATA-01 violation was found. The aspect classes and maximum-input value shape solve current requirements without a new framework. The normal-factory stamina reconciliation addresses the observed zero-character initialization seam, not an invented missing-object case. Optional save defaults handle actual optional fields. Source-required clamps remain mechanic rules, not extra protection. The gathering-source filter correctly excludes the actual generated `Spelunking6` FILLER item with no sources; it is not evidence that the six real associations can be absent. No additional YAGNI or DRY change is warranted.
- **Frontend coupling and description provenance:** No frontend row model, UI arithmetic, authored game-description sentence, copied description table, or speculative forecasting API was added. Chapters retain generated metadata and Item/Enemy owners. Player retains names and skill/EXP. Storage retains item ownership and location-separated stacks. The late preparation hook's name does not introduce frontend-specific records.

#### Spec

- **Concrete specification findings: none.** The selected implementation matches the approved plan's ownership, saved mappings, six real cave/page associations, four-slot chapter strides, generated formulas and artifact flag, saved-period reading allowance, per-character maximums, shared recharge, and saved-snapshot refill estimate. The maximum formula keeps the final floor and the outside-multiplier Trackometer, merit, and companion terms. It uses each contributor's prepared Specialty effect and the native recharge card level. Royal Armory owns its selected Outpost multiplier. Chapter preparation follows Sailing artifact preparation; inspection calculations follow late Meritocracy. No selected requirement was silently removed or expanded into Part 3, time simulation, rollover, performed reading, or producer repairs.
- **Plan review:** The approved plan was reviewed against standards rather than treated as an exception. No additional plan-design defect was found. The test-maintainability finding above applies to execution assertions, not a reason to add abstractions or reopen approved product/Part 1 decisions.

#### Mechanics and evidence for judging the plan

**Original source inspected:** The ready endpoint identified itself as `source: "offline"`, selected character `3`, and scene `ySnowD3`. A read-only expression reported `{title: "Jelly_Operator", version: "1.22"}`. Source queries used the following accessors without executing gameplay handlers:

| Evidence | Exact accessor and decisive finding |
|---|---|
| P2-G1 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_579._customBlock_Spelunk.toString()`: `ChapterBonus` reads `Spelunk[8][round(4 * chapter + bonus)]`. It evaluates the chapter formula, then multiplies by `1 + ArtifactBonus(35,0)/100` when original tuple position 4 equals 1. |
| P2-G2 | `idleon.getAttr("CustomLists").h.SpelunkChapters`: original tuples align with existing generated `func`, `x1`, `x2`, `x3`, and `requiredPages`. Generated `x3` is tuple position 4, the artifact-applicability flag. It is not missing static data. |
| P2-G3 | Same Spelunk helper: `MaxDailyPageReads` is `5 + 3 * RiftSkillBonus,18(4)`. Source Rift helper returns an unlock indicator; website Skill Mastery returns the full three extra reads. |
| P2-G4 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_38.prototype._event_InvItem4custom.toString()`: the `SPELUNKY_PAGE` branch checks shared usage at `OptionsListAccount[410]`, rejects stacks below 100 pages, selects a chapter through the page item's ID, checks the bonus's minimum stack size, increments shared usage once, and changes that bonus's shared level. This does not make storage or combined stacks directly readable. |
| P2-G5 | Spelunk helper: `StaminaMax` reads each character's own Spelunking level. Its full formula is recorded below. `ShopUpgBonus` leaves upgrades 4 and 5 linear, but multiplies upgrade 61 by saved `Spelunk[4][8]`. `OverstimBonus2` and Maxima-Track's generated description identify this as the Trackometer contribution. |
| P2-G6 | Spelunk helper: `StaminaRegenRate` has shared modifier inputs and no character-level lookup. It starts at five stamina per hour. Its card contribution uses native `CardLv("w7a10")`, capped after multiplication by two, not an equipment-boosted card effect. |
| P2-G7 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_713.prototype._customEvent_SpelunkStuff2.toString()`: conditional refill adds hourly rate times elapsed seconds divided by 3600, then caps saved stamina at its maximum. Excess can feed Overstim. The website estimate must not run those handlers, reconstruct team selection, or claim an active/away-time simulation. |
| P2-G8 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_345._customBlock_getbonus2.toString()`: argument `-1` chooses the maximum effect across characters. `(1,236,-1)` is the first effect of talent 236, Spelunking Specialty, not an achievement. Allocated talents use each contributing character's extra talent levels. |
| P2-G9 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_579._customBlock_RoyalG.toString()`: `OutpostROGbon(2,0)` returns 1 unless saved selected Outpost category is 2. If selected, it returns `1 + n / (40 + n)`, where `n = max(1, builtOutpostCount - 6)`. `TotalStatz(4,0)` counts built Outposts. |
| P2-G10 | `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_579._customBlock_SushiStuff.toString()`: `RoG_BonusQTY(11,0)` is the unlocked Sushi bonus. `idleon.allScripts.ActorEvents_579._customBlock_RiftStuff.toString()` confirms that mastery source rewards are indicators, while the existing website API supplies their full effect quantities. |

**Maximum-stamina grouping:** Let `level` be the character's nonnegative Spelunking level; `companion` be companion 148's active bonus; `fish` be Advice on Being Tired; `chapter(a,b)` be that owned Journal bonus.

```text
floor(
  upgrade61Raw * trackometerLevel
  + world7StaminaMerit
  + 10 * companion
  + (14 + level
     + upgrade4 * floor(level / 10)
     + chapter(2,0) + masteryMaxStamina
     + upgrade5 + chapter(3,0) + highestSpelunkingSpecialty)
    * (1 + fish / 100)
    * (1 + companion / 100)
)
```

The Trackometer, task merit, and additive companion terms sit outside the two multipliers. Source adds chapter `(3,0)` and the talent effect as flat terms despite their `%` descriptions. Changing their operation from description text would change the requested cap.

**Hourly-recharge grouping:**

```text
5
* (1 + meritocracy17 / 100)
* (1 + legendTalent30 / 100)
* max(1, royalOutpostRechargeMultiplier)
* (1 + sushi11 / 100)
* (1 + (upgrade5 + bubbleY11 + chapter(2,1) + chapter(5,2)
        + masteryRecharge + min(2 * nativeCardLevel, 20)) / 100)
```

This produces one shared rate in the inspected version. Characters still have distinct saved amounts and caps. Refill uses the requested `max(0, maximum - savedCurrent) / hourlyRecharge` estimate. No wall-clock catch-up or live countdown is planned.

### Latest nine-feedback simplification contract — 2026-10-14

This contract replaces the previous nine-point, six-point, and revision 4 presentation specifications where they conflict.
The [current card plan](spelunking-part-1-ui-plan.md) describes delivery. Approval, source provenance, and proof remain in this handoff.

| User feedback | Current contract and implementation |
|---|---|
| 1. Remove Grand tips | Grand found/capacity and Requirements met/not met or Collection complete remain plain text. Remove the information icon, Grand details, and all hidden/popover fallbacks. |
| 2. Simplify discovery tips | Found images are plain actual `IconImage` elements in 32px wrapping Boxes. Missing images retain opacity `0.55`; only they have a tooltip. Its heading is Spawn depth; its body is only `commaNotation(discovery.data.x6)`. No object-identity header or extra fields remain. |
| 3. Hide completed Boss depth | Use `!tunnel.loreCompleted`, not availability. Eight completed cards omit Boss depth. Scoutpost remains available/incomplete with `115`; Exiled Throne remains locked/incomplete with `130`. |
| 4. Strengthen Best depth | `TextAndLabel textSize="xlarge"` supplies the primary value with its default small label. Completed records use two equal columns; incomplete records use three. No empty placeholder remains. Raw values are unchanged. |
| 5. Match game Amber progression | `currentAmberDenomination` selects the highest active account tier. `AmberDenomination` owns image and normalized haul formatting. The human-approved backend, UI cutover, tests, TypeScript, independent review, and final Red-tier rendered proof are complete. |
| 6. Remove top help | Remove Display notes, Shared drops, their components/imports, and replacement or relocated help. Keep domain metadata and actual inline tunnel drops unchanged. |
| 7. Consolidate account totals | One row follows the heading. Its first two entries are Account discoveries and Account Grand Discoveries, with unfiltered found/capacity totals and stronger small numbers. |

The dropdown wrapper is fixed at 130px with `flex={false}`; Select uses `size="small"` and `aria-label="Collection status"`.
Account labels remain small/accent-2; numeric children are small with weight 500.

**Implemented Amber ownership:** Exported `AmberDenomination` and five frozen singleton definitions live in the existing Spelunking domain.
The denomination owns identity, base-unit divisor, optional upgrade gate, cached immutable 50×50 image data, and `formatAmount(baseAmount)`.
`Spelunking.currentAmberDenomination` selects the highest active tier using existing `getUpgradeBonus`, with Orange as the ungated default.
`SpelunkingDisplay` selects the owner once per render and passes it to each TunnelCard.
Each card delegates image, normalized amount, and unit name to the denomination. Its accessible label uses formatted amount plus denomination name.
The obsolete `getBaseAmberImageData()` API and sole UI call are removed, as is the UI `notateNumber` import.
No compatibility alias, new update hook, mutable tier cache, or per-render denomination copies were added.

| Tier | Denomination | Base Amber value | Activation through `getUpgradeBonus` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Orange Amber | `1` | Default |
| 1 | Blue Amber | `1e3` | Upgrade `20`, bonus at least `1` |
| 2 | Green Amber | `1e9` | Upgrade `41`, bonus at least `1` |
| 3 | Red Amber | `1e21` | Upgrade `51`, bonus at least `1` |
| 4 | Pink Amber | `1e36` | Upgrade `66`, bonus at least `1` |

### Part 1 maintainability revision

**Result:** All twelve approved feedback dispositions are implemented. Focused comparisons, TypeScript, and two fresh account-runtime passes succeeded. No implementation blocker remains. Stop before another review or commit.

This revision replaces the original plan's separately registered preparation hook, mutable requirement snapshots, both-side name normalization, and authored backend presentation sentences. The backend plan now describes direct metadata, cohesive aspect parsing, derived state, and representative tests. Approval of the earlier design was not treated as an exemption from maintainability feedback.

| Feedback | Implemented disposition |
|---|---|
| 1. Hat Rack ownership | Removed `Spelunking.premiumHatsFound` and its parsing/reset assignments. `HatRack.getDepositedHatCount()` returns the retained saved sequence length. Tome criterion 109 reads that owner without changing calculation order or filtering/deduplicating the count. |
| 2. Mechanic terminology | Developer comments explain Amber kept after exit settlement rather than naming an obfuscated source actor. Source identifiers remain in evidence references. |
| 3. Facts, not presentation prose | Removed `delveContext`, `recordContext`, descriptive requirement/drop strings, and `amberContext`. Retained intrinsic game descriptions and directly accessible exported reward/item metadata. |
| 4. Save-boundary normalization | Normalize underscores and surrounding whitespace in saved discovery names only. Use exported discovery, boss, and tunnel names unchanged. |
| 5. Minimal records and contracts | Removed copied object requirement interfaces and fields. `Discovery.data` and `Tunnel.data` retain generated metadata. Developer comments explain POW/HP/Amber inputs, depth scaling, first-object tunnel POW, raw spawn depths, displayed boss depth, and raw records. |
| 6. Aspect-owned parsing | Inspected Hole Well/Fountain parsing and followed its cohesive aspect pattern. `Tunnel.parse` owns its save section; no public per-assignment parsing methods remain. |
| 7. Stable test identity | Tests identify named tunnels and behavior. Fixture-specific selection reasons are separate comments rather than mutable saved counts in test titles. |
| 8. Developer comments | Added targeted comments for parse replacement, static ownership, Grand gate/rarity, and metadata rules. No website-facing explanatory sentences remain in the removed records. |
| 9. Required-state access | Required initialized upgrades use direct access. Removed empty released-catalogue fallbacks; retained future-slot filtering, optional save fields, and genuinely absent non-item metadata. |
| 10. Unnecessary lifecycle hook | Removed `updateSpelunking`, its import/registration, and domain/tunnel update/reset methods. Access, summaries, and Grand eligibility derive from authoritative state when requested. |
| 11. Redundant mutable projections | Removed copied availability/met flags, final-object ownership, remaining-capacity fields, and the shared mutable upgrade requirement. Grand gating uses the actual initialized Upgrade. Derived getters require no synchronized snapshot. |
| 12. Repeated comparisons | Replaced tenfold repeated behavioral/parameter assertions with representative cases and one compact all-index ordinary/Grand mapping comparison. Removed 15 unused extraction entries without changing retained expected values. |
